1	\subsection*{DDR Trend Lines}
2	
3	Figure~\ref{fig:ddr-trends} shows the per-feature depth trend (mean across all samples at each depth) with individual generator traces overlaid.
4	The consistent downward trajectories for drifting features across all generators confirm that DDR is a universal property of recursive generation.
5	Shape features exhibit flat trajectories regardless of generator, with near-zero $R^2$ for the linear fit.
6	$R^2$ values are computed from ordinary least-squares regression on depth-level means (five points per generator, one per depth 0--4), not per-sample regression; the annotated Spearman $\rho$ provides the complementary per-sample monotonicity measure.
7	
8	\begin{figure}[H]
9	\centering
10	\includegraphics[width=\columnwidth]{fig_appendix_ddr_trends.pdf}
11	\caption{Per-feature depth trends. Bold line: pooled mean ($\pm$1.96\,SE shading) across all generators, colored by DDR zone (blue: drifting, wine: intermediate, amber: near-stationary). Thin traces: individual generators. Spearman $\rho$ and linear $R^2$ are annotated per panel.}
12	\label{fig:ddr-trends}
13	\end{figure}
14	
15	\subsection*{UMAP Embedding}
16	
17	Figure~\ref{fig:umap} shows a 2D UMAP projection of the 7-feature space (7{,}500 stratified samples, StandardScaler normalization, $k$\,=\,30 neighbors, min\_dist\,=\,0.3).
18	Panel~(a) reveals a depth gradient from gen-0 through gen-4, with progressive overlap at deeper generations consistent with the convergence that motivates the gen-2+ class collapse.
19	Panel~(b) shows the 3-class view: gen-0 forms a separable cluster, while gen-1 and gen-2+ overlap substantially, explaining the classification difficulty at this boundary.
20	
21	\begin{figure}[H]
22	\centering
23	\includegraphics[width=\columnwidth, trim=0 0 356pt 0, clip]{fig_appendix_umap.pdf}\\[-2pt]
24	{\small (a) Colored by raw generation depth (0--4)}\\[6pt]
25	\includegraphics[width=\columnwidth, trim=356pt 0 0 0, clip]{fig_appendix_umap.pdf}\\[-2pt]
26	{\small (b) Colored by 3-class label}
27	\caption{UMAP 2D projection of the 7-feature space ($N$\,=\,7{,}500 stratified sample). (a) A gradient structure emerges, with deeper depths converging. (b) Gen-0 separates clearly; gen-1 and gen-2+ overlap, consistent with the high false-negative rate at the gen-1/gen-2+ boundary.}
28	\label{fig:umap}
29	\end{figure}
30	
```

{
  "recorded_role": "user",
  "parentUuid": "65e54b68-d2e4-46ff-a922-e5536c504bda",
  "isSidechain": false,
  "promptId": "01ed808c-c850-4acd-ba39-a096b467941a",
  "type": "user",
  "uuid": "e1f17445-f2d9-48e1-9e8f-f0d7ed3425f3",
  "toolUseResult": {
    "filePath": "/home/ubuntu/.agent-ml-research-idea_gen_0514_3/projects/gen-depth-fingerprint/docs/paper/introduction.tex",
    "oldString": "We term this location/shape asymmetry the \\emph{differential depth response} (DDR; Figure~\\ref{fig:feature-drift}), an empirical regularity",
    "newString": "We term this location/shape asymmetry the \\emph{differential depth response} (DDR; Figure~\\ref{fig:ddr-trajectory}), an empirical regularity",
    "originalFile": "\\section{Introduction}\n\\label{sec:introduction}\n\nAn estimated 7--17\\% of recent scientific publications already contain substantial LLM-modified content \\cite{mapping_llm_science,monitoring_ai_review}. As these outputs re-enter training corpora \\cite{dolma,fineweb} and seed further generation, text accumulates at varying \\emph{generation depths}; each successive cycle reshapes distributional properties in ways that matter for data provenance, corpus auditing, and contamination monitoring in base-model pretraining corpora \\cite{ccnet,dsir} as well as model collapse analysis \\cite{curse_of_recursion,strong_model_collapse}. Understanding \\emph{how} recursive generation alters text distributions---and which properties change versus which are preserved---is therefore a prerequisite for principled corpus curation, yet current methods address only binary human-or-machine detection \\cite{detectgpt,fast_detectgpt,binoculars,ld_score}.\nBase models provide the cleanest substrate to isolate depth effects from alignment-induced changes.\nWe find that recursive generation produces a decomposable depth profile: distributional \\emph{location} drifts with depth while \\emph{shape} remains stable across generators, an asymmetry sufficient for cross-generator depth prediction.\n\nDistributional compression under recursive generation is well-documented \\cite{curious_decline,broken_telephone,markovian_chains}, yet existing analyses characterize \\emph{that} and \\emph{why} convergence occurs without decomposing what drifts from what is preserved.\nWe introduce \\emph{depth profiling} (Figure~\\ref{fig:framework}), classifying text by ordinal generation depth (gen-0\\,/\\,gen-1\\,/\\,gen-2+), and decompose this compression into structurally distinct components: across eight base-model generators spanning five architectural families (0.6B--8B), the NLL distribution's central tendency (mean entropy (reference-model NLL), lexical diversity) drifts monotonically with depth while its shape (entropy extremes, tail structure) remains an order of magnitude more stable ($d{=}0.76$--$1.17$ vs.\\ $d < 0.10$ for three near-stationary features; entropy std is intermediate ($d{=}0.55$); boundary features show additional nuance; convergent effect-size measures confirm this partition).\nWe term this location/shape asymmetry the \\emph{differential depth response} (DDR; Figure~\\ref{fig:feature-drift}), an empirical regularity of base-model autoregressive generation that is mechanistically distinct from model collapse \\cite{curse_of_recursion}, which erodes tail coverage via weight updates; DDR instead analyzes fixed-parameter inference chains.\nThe stationary shape component provides the cross-generator reference needed for depth prediction without per-generator calibration.\n\n\\begin{figure*}[t!]\n\\centering\n\\includegraphics[width=\\textwidth]{fig2_framework.pdf}\n\\caption{Depth profiling framework. (a)~Recursive generation chain: a human-written seed (gen-0) is iteratively fed through a generator $G$ to produce text at successive depths; eight base-model LLMs across five families (0.6B--8B) serve as generators. (b)~Feature extraction: each text is scored by a fixed reference model (Pythia-410M), yielding five NLL statistics and two surface diversity measures (seven DDR features total). (c)~Leave-One-Generator-Out evaluation: XGBoost classifies each text into gen-0\\,/\\,gen-1\\,/\\,gen-2+ with one generator held out per fold.}\n\\label{fig:framework}\n\\end{figure*}\nWe focus on base (pretrained) models; instruction tuning degrades depth signal (Appendix~\\ref{app:instruct}).\n\nAs a proof of concept, a lightweight 7-feature ensemble informed by DDR achieves leak-free binary F1\\,=\\,0.694 under leave-one-generator-out evaluation, matching multi-detector ensembles at ${\\sim}2.4\\times$ lower cost; an end-to-end QLoRA classifier reaches 3-class F1\\,=\\,0.905 (6-gen LOGO; Appendix~\\ref{app:qlora}), confirming that the depth signal extends well beyond handcrafted features.\n\nOur contributions:\n\\begin{enumerate}[nosep,leftmargin=*]\n    \\item \\textbf{Empirical decomposition (DDR).} Across eight generators from five families, we decompose inference-chain drift into a location component that shifts monotonically ($d{=}0.76$--$1.17$) and a shape component that remains near-stationary ($d < 0.10$ for three near-stationary features). Where prior work documents \\emph{what converges} \\cite{curious_decline,markovian_chains}, DDR identifies \\emph{what is approximately preserved} and enables depth detection without per-generator calibration. DDR is a descriptive empirical finding; the classification value lies in this decomposition rather than the classifier itself.\n    \\item \\textbf{Task formulation and evaluation suite.} We define generation depth classification as an ordinal prediction task and construct a controlled dataset of 37K texts from eight generators across five families (0.6B--8B), with leave-one-generator-out cross-validation as the primary evaluation protocol.\n    \\item \\textbf{Scope characterization.} Systematic evaluation across temperature, prompt type, instruction tuning, and model scale maps the operational envelope of depth profiling, identifying key challenges in instruction-tuned models, cross-domain transfer, and perturbation robustness.\n\\end{enumerate}\n",
    "structuredPatch": [
      {
        "oldStart": 7,
        "oldLines": 7,
        "newStart": 7,
        "newLines": 7,

          " ",
          " Distributional compression under recursive generation is well-documented \\cite{curious_decline,broken_telephone,markovian_chains}, yet existing analyses characterize \\emph{that} and \\emph{why} convergence occurs without decomposing what drifts from what is preserved.",
          " We introduce \\emph{depth profiling} (Figure~\\ref{fig:framework}), classifying text by ordinal generation depth (gen-0\\,/\\,gen-1\\,/\\,gen-2+), and decompose this compression into structurally distinct components: across eight base-model generators spanning five architectural families (0.6B--8B), the NLL distribution's central tendency (mean entropy (reference-model NLL), lexical diversity) drifts monotonically with depth while its shape (entropy extremes, tail structure) remains an order of magnitude more stable ($d{=}0.76$--$1.17$ vs.\\ $d < 0.10$ for three near-stationary features; entropy std is intermediate ($d{=}0.55$); boundary features show additional nuance; convergent effect-size measures confirm this partition).",
          "-We term this location/shape asymmetry the \\emph{differential depth response} (DDR; Figure~\\ref{fig:feature-drift}), an empirical regularity of base-model autoregressive generation that is mechanistically distinct from model collapse \\cite{curse_of_recursion}, which erodes tail coverage via weight updates; DDR instead analyzes fixed-parameter inference chains.",
          "+We term this location/shape asymmetry the \\emph{differential depth response} (DDR; Figure~\\ref{fig:ddr-trajectory}), an empirical regularity of base-model autoregressive generation that is mechanistically distinct from model collapse \\cite{curse_of_recursion}, which erodes tail coverage via weight updates; DDR instead analyzes fixed-parameter inference chains.",

75	Appendix~\ref{app:qualitative} illustrates the DDR with a concrete generation chain.
76	The primary partition is binary: $k{=}2$ clustering cleanly separates three drifting from four non-drifting features; higher $k$ degrades quality ($k{=}3$: silhouette\,=\,0.532; Appendix~\ref{app:ddr-validation}).
77	A domain-informed refinement resolves entropy std as a sole \emph{intermediate} member ($d{=}0.55$, $|\rho|{=}0.16$), distinct from the three near-stationary features ($d < 0.10$; CV shift $<$3\% vs.\ $>$15\% for drifting features).
78	Multiple effect-size measures corroborate this partition (Quantification below).
79	
80	Pooled Spearman correlations with depth corroborate the binary split (Figure~\ref{fig:feature-drift}):
81	drifting features show substantial monotonic trends (hapax ratio $\rho = -0.64$, TTR $\rho = -0.63$, entropy mean $\rho = -0.60$),
82	while non-drifting features remain near-zero (tail decay $|\rho| = 0.04$; per-depth distributions in Appendix~\ref{app:feature-distributions}).
83	The six-fold magnitude gap between drifting ($|\rho| > 0.60$) and non-drifting features ($|\rho| \leq 0.16$) defines the DDR partition.
84	Within the non-drifting group, entropy std ($|\rho| = 0.16$, $d{=}0.55$) shows a substantial level shift despite low monotonicity, distinguishing it from the three near-stationary features ($d < 0.10$); this refinement is stable across $d$ thresholds in $[0.56, 0.75]$ (Appendix~\ref{app:ddr-validation}).
85	
86	\paragraph{Quantification.}
87	Parametric and non-parametric effect-size measures converge on the split.
88	Cohen's $d$ \cite{cohen1988statistical} shows a ${\geq}7\times$ gap between drifting ($d{=}0.76$--$1.17$) and near-stationary features ($d \leq 0.08$); KS statistics, Glass's $\Delta$, Cliff's $\delta$, and CV shift all corroborate (Appendix~\ref{app:ddr-validation}), with the partition robust under heteroscedasticity (Glass's $\Delta$ gap ${\geq}9.5\times$; Appendix~\ref{app:heteroscedasticity}).
89	Four features fall cleanly into drifting or non-drifting groups.
```

The last visible text on page 8 right column is: "DDR is mechanistically distinct from model collapse (Shumailov et al., 2024; Dohmatob et al., 2025)—retraining erodes tail coverage via weight updates, whereas inference-chain drift shifts only location—and complements Mohamed"


Page 9 continues the Discussion with citations and comparisons to related work, then wraps up with the Conclusion section. I'm seeing about 8-9 lines of overflow total—roughly half a column inch—so I need to trim the Discussion and Conclusion sections to fit everything by page 9.

Looking at the Discussion text, I can merge the three paragraphs into two by combining related ideas about the empirical regularity of autoregressive generation and the ablation results, which should save several lines without losing the core argument. DDR operates through a distinct mechanism from model collapse—retraining degrades tail coverage via weight updates while inference-chain drift only shifts the distribution's location—and it complements prior work on factuality degradation and convergence dynamics by decomposing location versus shape effects, with all three phenomena plateauing around 3–5 iterations. Instruction tuning reduces DDR but QLoRA restores strong performance, and the signal generalizes across domains despite the compression. I'm looking at the Results section now to find more compression opportunities—the Validation paragraph is particularly dense, and Feature complementarity could be tightened. I'll also increase the vspace reductions throughout, trying \vspace{-3mm} at section headers and adding \vspace{-1mm} after key paragraphs to reclaim those extra lines needed to hit 8 pages.

```text
1	\vspace{-2mm}
2	\section{Discussion}
3	\label{sec:discussion}
4	\vspace{-2mm}
5	
6	%% Mechanism — why DDR exists
7	\looseness=-1 We hypothesize that DDR reflects an empirical regularity of base-model autoregressive generation: recursive inputs compress the token-level NLL distribution monotonically (Appendix~\ref{app:nll-distribution}), producing location drift independent of the token-selection mechanism.
8	Controlled ablations support this: greedy decoding preserves the full partition (Appendix~\ref{app:decoding-strategy}), temperature modulates drift magnitude while preserving the partition (Appendix~\ref{app:sampling-sensitivity}), and semantic-constraint attenuation confirms predictions (Appendix~\ref{app:prompt-type}).
9	ANOVA quantifies the asymmetry: $\eta^2_{\text{depth}}{=}0.34$--$0.37$ for location vs.\ ${<}0.01$ for shape (Appendix~\ref{app:variance-decomposition}).
10	
11	%% Implications — what DDR enables
12	\looseness=-1 Depth constitutes a new analytical dimension for corpus auditing; although per-instance gen-2+ recall is limited (${\sim}$27\%), batch contamination estimation reaches ${\pm}3$--$4$\% at $n{=}1{,}000$ (Appendix~\ref{app:error-analysis}), sufficient for auditing where the goal is contamination proportion rather than per-text judgment.
13	
14	%% Scope \& Connections — where DDR works and fails
15	\looseness=-1 DDR is mechanistically distinct from model collapse \cite{curse_of_recursion,strong_model_collapse}---retraining erodes tail coverage via weight updates, whereas inference-chain drift shifts only location---and complements \citeauthor{broken_telephone}'s factuality degradation and \citeauthor{markovian_chains}'s convergence dynamics by adding location/shape decomposition; all three observe saturation at ${\sim}$3--5 iterations (Appendix~\ref{app:iterative-comparison}).
16	Instruction tuning compresses DDR, though QLoRA recovers F1\,=\,0.89--0.91 (Appendix~\ref{app:instruct}); cross-domain signal persists at F1\,=\,0.74--0.75 (Appendix~\ref{app:domain}).
17	\vspace{-2mm}
18	
```

</details>

  "content": "\\vspace{-3mm}\n\\section{Discussion}\n\\label{sec:discussion}\n\\vspace{-2mm}\n\n\\looseness=-1 We hypothesize that DDR reflects an empirical regularity of base-model autoregressive generation: recursive inputs compress the token-level NLL distribution monotonically (Appendix~\\ref{app:nll-distribution}), producing location drift independent of the token-selection mechanism.\nControlled ablations support this: greedy decoding preserves the full partition (Appendix~\\ref{app:decoding-strategy}), temperature modulates drift magnitude while preserving the partition (Appendix~\\ref{app:sampling-sensitivity}), and semantic-constraint attenuation confirms predictions (Appendix~\\ref{app:prompt-type}).\nANOVA quantifies the asymmetry: $\\eta^2_{\\text{depth}}{=}0.34$--$0.37$ for location vs.\\ ${<}0.01$ for shape (Appendix~\\ref{app:variance-decomposition}).\n\n\\looseness=-1 Depth constitutes a new analytical dimension for corpus auditing; batch contamination estimation reaches ${\\pm}3$--$4$\\% at $n{=}1{,}000$ (Appendix~\\ref{app:error-analysis}).\nDDR is mechanistically distinct from model collapse \\cite{curse_of_recursion,strong_model_collapse}---retraining erodes tail via weight updates, whereas inference-chain drift shifts only location---and complements concurrent iterative-generation studies (Appendix~\\ref{app:iterative-comparison}).\nInstruction tuning compresses DDR, though QLoRA recovers F1\\,=\\,0.89--0.91 (Appendix~\\ref{app:instruct}); cross-domain signal persists at F1\\,=\\,0.74--0.75 (Appendix~\\ref{app:domain}).\n\\vspace{-2mm}\n"

```text
95	Permutation testing confirms the split as non-random ($p{=}0.030$); reclassifying either boundary feature preserves the core split.
96	
97	\paragraph{Feature complementarity.}
98	The full 7-feature set (F1\,=\,0.773, 10-fold stratified CV, complementing the LOGO protocol in Table~\ref{tab:logo-results}) outperforms surface-only (0.688) and reference-only (0.713; McNemar $p \approx 0$; Appendix~\ref{app:ablation-table}).
99	The collinear drifting triplet captures ${\sim}$1 effective dimension (PCA PC1\,=\,92.3\%; Appendix~\ref{sec:appendix-feature-correlation}); group ablation confirms complementarity for depth profiling: 3-class F1 gains $+$5.3\,pp when non-drifting features are added (drifting-only 0.716, all-7 0.769), while binary detection is already saturated by location drift alone (drifting-only binary F1\,=\,0.697 vs.\ all-7\,=\,0.700; Appendix~\ref{app:ablation-table})---shape stability contributes to depth \emph{grading}, not detection.
100	TTR and hapax ratio ($r = 0.97$) are retained for marginal LOFO gains \cite{strobl_conditional_2008} (Appendix~\ref{app:lofo}).
101	
102	\paragraph{Cross-family reference models.}
103	To isolate reference-model bias, we evaluate on a 5-generator subset excluding all Pythia-family generators---a stricter protocol than 8-gen LOGO.
104	Alternative references (OLMo-2-1B, Qwen3-0.6B) achieve comparable performance (F1 spread\,=\,0.013; Table~\ref{tab:cross-ref} in Appendix~\ref{app:cross-ref}), with drift direction consistently negative.
105	Same-family proximity may inflate Pythia F1 by up to 1.3\,pp, but the DDR partition is fully preserved regardless of reference choice.
106	
107	\paragraph{Temperature and prompt-type sensitivity.}
108	All four tested generators degrade at $t{=}0.9$ ($-$9.3 to $-$26.0\,pp binary F1; Appendix~\ref{app:sampling-sensitivity}); continuation chains show the strongest drift (F1\,=\,0.781; Appendix~\ref{app:prompt-type}).
109	The DDR partition hierarchy is preserved across temperatures (Appendix~\ref{app:ddr-temp-stability}).
110	
111	
112	\subsection{Comparison with Detection Baselines}
113	\label{sec:results-baselines}
114	
115	We next ask whether existing detection methods---designed for binary real-vs-synthetic classification---incidentally capture depth structure, repurposing four detection scores as ordinal depth predictors under identical XGBoost and LOGO conditions (Table~\ref{tab:baselines}).
116	The strongest baseline is a 4-detector + surface feature ensemble (binary F1\,=\,0.708), which pools four detection scores with TTR and hapax ratio; among individual detectors, Avg-LogProb provides the most meaningful single-feature comparison.
117	
118	\begin{table}[t]
119	\centering
120	\footnotesize
121	\caption{Depth classification (8-gen LOGO, pooled evaluation; pooled F1 is slightly higher than per-generator macro F1 in Table~\ref{tab:logo-results} because larger test folds from high-performing generators contribute proportionally more). Identical XGBoost configuration. 7-feature: one 410M model (905\,MB); 4-det ensembles: two models, 1.4B params (2{,}868\,MB). Individual detection scores (N/A) share the reference-model forward pass; per-method timing is not separately profiled.}
122	\label{tab:baselines}
123	\begin{tabular}{lcccc}
124	\toprule
125	\textbf{Method} & \textbf{Feat.} & \textbf{3-class F1} & \textbf{Bin F1} & \textbf{ms/text} \\
126	\midrule
127	4-det.\ + surface     & 6 & \textbf{0.795} & \textbf{0.708} & 22.8 \\
128	4-detector ens.\      & 4 & 0.785 & 0.700 & 22.8 \\
129	7-feature (ours)      & 7 & 0.774 & 0.705 & \textbf{9.7} \\
130	\midrule
131	Avg-LogProb           & 1 & 0.707 & 0.673 & --- \\
132	FastDetectGPT         & 1 & 0.660 & 0.569 & --- \\
133	LD-Score              & 1 & 0.630 & 0.675 & --- \\
134	Binoculars            & 1 & 0.411 & 0.539 & --- \\
135	\bottomrule
136	\end{tabular}
137	\end{table}
138	
139	The 7-feature ensemble matches the 4-detector+surface ensemble (binary F1: 0.705 vs.\ 0.708; Figure~\ref{fig:baseline-ci}) at ${\sim}2.4\times$ lower cost, surpassing all individual detectors by 3.0--16.6\,pp.
140	DDR's contribution is the decomposition into location-sensitive and shape-stable components (\S\ref{sec:discussion}), with classification performance comparable to baseline ensembles rather than a performance advance.
141	Ordinal-specific methods (CORAL, Frank-Hall) yield comparable F1 under this setup (Appendix~\ref{app:classifier}), suggesting limited advantage from ranking-aware losses at 3-class resolution.
142

The text continues and I can see at the very bottom right: "Depth constitutes a new analytical dimension for corpus auditing; batch contamination estimation reaches ±3–4% at n=1,000 (Appendix l). DDR is mechanistically distinct from model collapse (Shumailov et al., 2024; Dohmatob et al., 2025)— retraining erodes tail via weight updates, whereas inference-chain drift shifts only location—and complements concurrent iterative-generation stud-ies (Appendix z.7). 

The Discussion continues with findings about how instruction tuning compresses DDR, but QLoRA can recover strong performance metrics around F1 = 0.89–0.91 with the effect persisting across domains at F1 = 0.74–0.75. Page 9 picks up with just those final performance numbers before moving into the Conclusion section.

The Conclusion describes how recursive generation creates a decomposable depth response where location features shift while shape remains stable, allowing cross-generator depth classification with F1 scores between 0.694–0.762, though extending this to instruction-tuned models is still an open question.

I'm looking at the page layout and realizing I only have about 4-5 lines of main text spilling onto page 9, which is very close to fitting. I could save that space by reducing vertical spacing in the Results section—particularly before the DDR subsection and the baselines subsection—or by compressing the Discussion further, or even tightening the Conclusion line itself.

Let me try a combination of small adjustments: adding negative vspace before key subsections, removing the ANOVA sentence from Discussion since it's somewhat redundant, and possibly condensing the "Ordinal-specific methods" sentence in the baselines section. The Discussion is already quite tight, so I might also consider moving the last sentence to a footnote or cutting it entirely if needed. I'm checking how much vertical space is available before the Results section to see if I can reduce margins there.

1	\section{Results}
2	\label{sec:results}
3	
4	\begin{figure*}[t]
5	\centering
6	\includegraphics[width=\textwidth]{fig_ddr_trajectory.pdf}
7	\caption{Feature trajectories across generation depth ($y$-axis: $z$-scored feature values). \textbf{Top row (drifting)}: entropy mean and hapax ratio decline monotonically across all eight generators ($|\rho| > 0.59$). \textbf{Bottom row (stationary)}: entropy max and tail decay remain flat ($|\rho| < 0.07$). Colored lines: individual generators; diamonds: cross-generator mean (blue for drifting, amber for stationary). This dichotomy defines the DDR.}
8	\label{fig:ddr-trajectory}
9	\end{figure*}
10	
11	\subsection{Depth Classification Performance}
12	\label{sec:results-classification}
13	
14	The 7-feature ensemble achieves 3-class LOGO F1\,=\,0.762 (95\% CI [0.758, 0.766]; $N$\,=\,37{,}058) across eight generators from five families (Table~\ref{tab:logo-results}), with cross-generator F1 spread of 9.1\,pp capturing generator selection uncertainty.
15	This 3-class metric is inflated by shared gen-0 seeds across generators (per-class gen-0 F1\,$\approx$\,0.94; \S\ref{sec:generation}); we therefore report \textbf{leak-free binary F1} (gen-1 vs.\ gen-2+)\,=\,0.694 (CI [0.688, 0.700]) as the primary metric throughout, excluding gen-0 entirely.
```

    "oldString": "Instruction tuning compresses DDR, though QLoRA recovers F1\\,=\\,0.89--0.91 (Appendix~\\ref{app:instruct}); cross-domain signal persists at F1\\,=\\,0.74--0.75 (Appendix~\\ref{app:domain}).",
    "newString": "Instruction tuning compresses DDR (QLoRA: F1\\,=\\,0.89--0.91; Appendix~\\ref{app:instruct}); cross-domain signal persists (F1\\,=\\,0.74--0.75; Appendix~\\ref{app:domain}).",
    "originalFile": "\\vspace{-3mm}\n\\section{Discussion}\n\\label{sec:discussion}\n\\vspace{-2mm}\n\n\\looseness=-1 We hypothesize that DDR reflects an empirical regularity of base-model autoregressive generation: recursive inputs compress the token-level NLL distribution monotonically (Appendix~\\ref{app:nll-distribution}), producing location drift independent of the token-selection mechanism.\nControlled ablations support this: greedy decoding preserves the full partition (Appendix~\\ref{app:decoding-strategy}), temperature modulates drift magnitude while preserving the partition (Appendix~\\ref{app:sampling-sensitivity}), and semantic-constraint attenuation confirms predictions (Appendix~\\ref{app:prompt-type}).\nANOVA quantifies the asymmetry: $\\eta^2_{\\text{depth}}{=}0.34$--$0.37$ for location vs.\\ ${<}0.01$ for shape (Appendix~\\ref{app:variance-decomposition}).\n\n\\looseness=-1 Depth constitutes a new analytical dimension for corpus auditing; batch contamination estimation reaches ${\\pm}3$--$4$\\% at $n{=}1{,}000$ (Appendix~\\ref{app:error-analysis}).\nDDR is mechanistically distinct from model collapse \\cite{curse_of_recursion,strong_model_collapse}---retraining erodes tail via weight updates, whereas inference-chain drift shifts only location---and complements concurrent iterative-generation studies (Appendix~\\ref{app:iterative-comparison}).\nInstruction tuning compresses DDR, though QLoRA recovers F1\\,=\\,0.89--0.91 (Appendix~\\ref{app:instruct}); cross-domain signal persists at F1\\,=\\,0.74--0.75 (Appendix~\\ref{app:domain}).\n\\vspace{-2mm}\n",

    "newString": "\\vspace{-1mm}\n\\section{Method}\n\\label{sec:method}",
    "originalFile": "\\section{Method}\n\\label{sec:method}\n\n\\begin{table}[t!]\n\\centering\n\\footnotesize\n\\caption{Seven distributional features. ``Entropy'' features are computed as per-token NLL under Pythia-410M (see \\S\\ref{sec:features}); surface features require no reference model. DDR analysis (\\S\\ref{sec:results-differential}) reveals that entropy mean and the two surface features (TTR, hapax ratio) drift with depth while entropy max, min, and tail decay remain near-stationary; entropy std is intermediate. All ``entropy'' features are reference-model NLL values (cross-entropy with Pythia-410M), not information-theoretic entropy; see \\S\\ref{sec:features}.}\n\\label{tab:features}\n\\setlength{\\tabcolsep}{4pt}\n\\begin{tabular}{@{}llp{3.0cm}@{}}\n\\toprule\n\\textbf{Feature} & \\textbf{Source} & \\textbf{Measures} \\\\\n\\midrule\n\\texttt{entropy\\_mean} & Ref.\\ NLL & Central tendency of token surprise \\\\\n\\texttt{entropy\\_std}  & Ref.\\ NLL & Spread of token surprise \\\\\n\\texttt{entropy\\_max}  & Ref.\\ NLL & Maximum token surprise \\\\\n\\texttt{entropy\\_min}  & Ref.\\ NLL & Minimum token surprise \\\\\n\\texttt{tail\\_decay}   & Ref.\\ NLL & Log-prob decay slope (ranks 100--1K) \\\\\n\\midrule\n\\texttt{ttr}           & Surface & Type-token ratio \\\\\n\\texttt{hapax\\_ratio}  & Surface & Proportion of hapax legomena \\\\\n\\bottomrule\n\\end{tabular}\n\\end{table}\n\n\\subsection{Task Formulation}\n\\label{sec:task}\n\nGiven a text $x$, we predict its \\emph{generation depth} $d \\in \\{0, 1, 2{+}\\}$: human-written ($d{=}0$), first-generation LLM output ($d{=}1$), or text produced by two or more recursive LLM generations ($d{=}2{+}$).\nA generation chain of depth $k$ is defined recursively: $x^{(0)} \\sim \\mathcal{C}_0$ (human corpus), $x^{(k)} = G(x^{(k-1)})$ for $k \\geq 1$, where $G$ denotes stochastic autoregressive generation.\nDepths beyond 2 are merged into $2{+}$ because feature convergence compresses inter-class margins at deeper levels (\\S\\ref{sec:results-differential}).\n\n\\subsection{Generation Chain Construction}\n\\label{sec:generation}\n\nWe construct generation chains using eight generators spanning five model families: \\textbf{Pythia} (2.8B, 1B) \\cite{pythia}, \\textbf{OLMo} (OLMo-2-1B) \\cite{olmo}, \\textbf{Qwen} (Qwen3-1.7B, Qwen3-0.6B, Qwen3-8B-Base) \\cite{qwen3}, \\textbf{Llama} (Llama-3.2-3B) \\cite{llama3}, and \\textbf{Mistral} (Mistral-7B-v0.3) \\cite{mistral7b}.\nThese generators span five distinct architectures with a 13$\\times$ parameter range (0.6B--8B), trained on heterogeneous corpora; Qwen contributes three models (0.6B--8B) covering the full scale range; Qwen's overrepresentation (3/8 generators) is a limitation, but LOFO holding out all three Qwen models (F1\\,=\\,0.686) provides the strictest family-level test.\n\nFor each generator, we sample approximately 1{,}000 seed passages from English Wikipedia as $\\mathcal{C}_0$.\nThe same pool of seed passages serves as gen-0 for all generators.\nWe use shared seeds across generators so that cross-generator comparisons control for input content and isolate the effect of the generation model from source text variation.\nIn LOGO evaluation, gen-0 samples from the held-out generator's chains also appear (with the same text) in the training generators' chains, creating a potential train--test overlap for the gen-0 class.\nWe address this with a supplementary binary evaluation excluding gen-0 entirely (\\S\\ref{sec:results-baselines}).\nEach seed is iteratively fed through the generator to produce a chain of depth 4 (i.e., gen-0 through gen-4), using the prompt ``\\texttt{Continue the following text: \\{text\\}}''.\nWe generate up to depth~4 to empirically confirm convergence: 5-class analysis shows adjacent depths 2--4 become indistinguishable (F1\\,$\\approx$\\,0.27; Appendix~\\ref{app:finer-depth}), motivating the gen-2+ class collapse.\nAll generation uses temperature $\\tau{=}0.7$, nucleus sampling with $p{=}0.9$, and maximum length of 256 new tokens.\nAfter filtering chain breaks and passages too short for feature extraction, 37{,}058 texts remain across all depths (Appendix~\\ref{app:dataset-stats}); depths 2--4 are collapsed into $2{+}$.\n\n\\subsection{Feature Extraction}\n\\label{sec:features}\n\n\\paragraph{Terminology.} Throughout this paper, we refer to per-token cross-entropy loss under a fixed reference model as ``entropy'' for conciseness; these are reference-model NLL values, not true information-theoretic entropy of the generating distribution.\n\nWe deliberately use interpretable distributional statistics so that the DDR partition remains transparent; end-to-end neural features improve classification ($+$11.6\\,pp; Appendix~\\ref{app:qlora}) but obscure the decomposition.\nLeave-one-feature-out analysis (Appendix~\\ref{app:lofo}) confirms that both reference-model and surface feature groups contribute independently.\n\nWe extract seven distributional features per text (Table~\\ref{tab:features}), selected to provide principled coverage of the NLL distribution---location (mean), spread (std), extremes (max, min), and tail structure (decay)---complemented by two reference-free surface diversity measures.\nWe evaluated six additional NLL statistics (median, 90th percentile, IQR, CV, skewness, kurtosis) as candidate features; all exhibit $|r| > 0.65$ with existing features and yield no classification gain when added ($\\Delta$F1\\,$\\leq$\\,$-0.011$; Appendix~\\ref{app:feature-candidate}).\n\n\\paragraph{Reference-model features.}\nA held-out reference model (Pythia-410M, chosen to be smaller than all generators) computes per-token negative log-likelihood (NLL) for each text.\nAlthough Pythia-410M shares the GPT-NeoX architecture with two of the eight generators, cross-family reference validation (Appendix~\\ref{app:cross-ref}) shows F1 spread of only 0.013 across Pythia, OLMo, and Qwen references, confirming DDR is reference-model agnostic; however, all tested references are causal LMs.\nFrom the resulting NLL sequence, we extract five statistics: mean, standard deviation, maximum, and minimum of the token-level NLL, plus tail\\_decay---the slope of the vocabulary-level averaged log-probability distribution in ranks 100--1000, capturing the power-law regime where depth-induced compression is most detectable while avoiding high-frequency head noise and sparse extreme-tail counts.\n\n\\paragraph{Surface features.}\nTwo features capture lexical diversity without requiring a reference model: type-token ratio (TTR) and the proportion of words appearing exactly once (hapax\\_ratio).\n\n\\paragraph{Feature complementarity.}\nThese features exhibit a \\emph{differential depth response} (DDR), separating into drifting and non-drifting groups (\\S\\ref{sec:results-differential}; pairwise correlations in Appendix~\\ref{sec:appendix-feature-correlation}).\nTTR and hapax ratio are strongly correlated ($r = 0.97$); both are retained for marginal LOFO gains.\n\n\\subsection{Evaluation Protocol}\n\\label{sec:evaluation}\n\n\\paragraph{Cross-validation.}\nWe evaluate generalization via \\textbf{Leave-One-Generator-Out (LOGO)} cross-validation: each fold holds out one generator entirely, so the classifier must generalize to a generator absent from training---the strictest per-model protocol for assessing deployment against previously unseen generators.\nLOGO does not isolate family-level effects; \\textbf{Leave-One-Family-Out (LOFO)} addresses this by holding out entire model families (binary F1\\,=\\,0.686; \\S\\ref{sec:results-classification}).\nGenerator subsets vary across experiments (Appendix~\\ref{app:gen-subsets-section}).\n\n\\paragraph{Classifier.}\nWe use XGBoost \\cite{xgboost} throughout ($n\\_\\text{estimators}{=}200$, $\\max\\_\\text{depth}{=}6$, learning rate $0.1$; fixed without tuning), with identical hyperparameters across all methods in Table~\\ref{tab:baselines}.\nAlternative classifiers (CORAL \\cite{coral_ordinal}, CORN \\cite{corn_ordinal}, Frank-Hall \\cite{frank_hall_ordinal}) yield comparable performance (Appendix~\\ref{app:classifier}); discriminative power resides in the features, not the classifier.\n\n\\paragraph{Metrics and statistical testing.}\nThe primary metric is \\textbf{macro-averaged F1} over the three depth classes, with 95\\% bootstrap CIs ($n{=}1{,}000$).\nAll $p$-values are two-sided with Bonferroni correction within each family of simultaneous tests.\n\n\\paragraph{Baselines.}\nWe compare the 7-feature ensemble against four single-score detection methods repurposed as depth predictors: Avg-LogProb, FastDetectGPT \\cite{fast_detectgpt}, Binoculars \\cite{binoculars}, and LD-Score \\cite{ld_score}.\nEach baseline score is used as a univariate feature in the same XGBoost framework under LOGO cross-validation to ensure a controlled comparison.\n\n\\paragraph{Length confound control.}\nSince TTR and hapax\\_ratio are mechanically length-dependent, we verify depth signal is not a length artifact via length-matched subsampling that equalizes length distributions across depth classes (\\S\\ref{sec:results-classification}; Appendix~\\ref{app:length}).\n",

```text
89	\paragraph{Quantification.}
90	Parametric and non-parametric effect-size measures converge on the split.
91	Cohen's $d$ \cite{cohen1988statistical} shows a ${\geq}7\times$ gap between drifting ($d{=}0.76$--$1.17$) and near-stationary features ($d \leq 0.08$); KS statistics, Glass's $\Delta$, Cliff's $\delta$, and CV shift all corroborate (Appendix~\ref{app:ddr-validation}), with the partition robust under heteroscedasticity (Glass's $\Delta$ gap ${\geq}9.5\times$; Appendix~\ref{app:heteroscedasticity}).
92	Four features fall cleanly into drifting or non-drifting groups.
93	Entropy min and tail decay are \emph{boundary features}: entropy min is near-stationary for gen-1 vs.\ gen-2+ ($d{=}0.016$) but sensitive at gen-0/gen-1 ($|\rho|{=}0.40$); tail decay is \emph{weakly stationary}, passing TOST for only 4/8 generators ($|d|{=}0.10$--$0.22$).
94	Entropy std ($d{=}0.55$, $|\rho|{=}0.16$) is the sole intermediate member.
95	\paragraph{Validation.}
96	Per-generator split analysis provides the strongest validation: each of the eight hold-out generators independently recovers the identical drifting/non-drifting partition (per-generator silhouette range 0.505--0.760, invariant across $\varepsilon \in [0.10, 0.75]$; Appendix~\ref{app:ddr-validation}).
97	TOST equivalence testing confirms near-stationarity for entropy min (8/8 pass) and entropy max (6/8); tail decay passes for only 4/8 ($|d|{=}0.10$--$0.22$), reflecting generator-specific tail structure variation rather than systematic drift: the effect sizes remain an order of magnitude below the drifting group ($d \geq 0.76$), and the partition assignment is unchanged.
98	Permutation testing confirms the split as non-random ($p{=}0.030$); reclassifying either boundary feature preserves the core split.
99	
100	\paragraph{Feature complementarity.}
```

</details>

### Tool result

_source line 6998 · 2026-05-22T17:07:23.245Z_

<details><summary>↩ Tool result · `toolu_01NHrRyAz5ArxhaEgr2Niazm`</summary>

````text
1	---
2	description: 用 PaperBanana 生成学术图表——概念图（架构/流程/framework）首选，探索性统计图也可。LLM 写 matplotlib 代码 + VLM Critic 闭环迭代。需要 GOOGLE_API_KEY。
3	name: paper-banana
4	scope: global
5	tags:
6	  - visualization
7	  - paper
8	  - matplotlib
9	  - llm-codegen
10	  - conceptual-diagram
11	version: 2
12	tools:
13	  - ssh_execute
14	---
15	
16	# Paper Banana — LLM 驱动的学术图表生成
17	
18	PaperBanana 由 Gemini 自动写 matplotlib 代码，VLM Critic 评估渲染结果并迭代优化（默认 3 轮），输出 NeurIPS 风格 300 DPI 图表。
19	
20	## 适用范围（与 paper-visualization.skill.md L4 路由一致）
21	
22	| 场景 | 推荐工具 | 原因 |
23	|------|---------|------|
24	| **概念图**（架构图、流程图、framework 示意） | **PaperBanana** | matplotlib 手写 boxes/arrows/icons 极痛苦，LLM 生成代码更灵活 |
25	| **统计图**（scatter、bar、line、heatmap、forest） | **matplotlib 手写** | 确定性、可控、有模板 |
26	| **探索性图**，不确定 layout | **PaperBanana** | LLM 多轮迭代试错 |
27	| 离线 / 无 GOOGLE_API_KEY | **matplotlib 手写** | 不依赖外部 API |
28	
29	> **核心定位**：PaperBanana 的真正优势是**概念图**——这类图没有数据输入、是纯视觉布局，手写 matplotlib 代码极其繁琐。统计图有成熟模板（见 `draw-paper-figure.skill.md`），手写更可控。
30	>
31	> **与 draw-paper-figure 的区别**：draw-paper-figure 是手写模板（快、可控、确定性）；paper-banana 是 LLM 自动生成代码（慢、灵活、适合新图类型）。
32	
33	## 前置条件：GOOGLE_API_KEY
34	
35	```bash
36	# 检查 key 是否已配置
37	ssh_execute("echo $GOOGLE_API_KEY | head -c 10")
38	
39	# 如未配置，需要用户提供 key，然后：
40	ssh_execute("echo 'export GOOGLE_API_KEY=[REDACTED]' >> ~/.bashrc && source ~/.bashrc")
41	
42	# 安装 PaperBanana
43	ssh_execute("pip install paperbanana -q")
44	
45	# 验证安装
46	ssh_execute("paperbanana --version")
47	```
48	
49	**⚠️ 如果 GOOGLE_API_KEY 未设置，直接停下来问用户要 key，不要尝试绕过。**
50	
51	**⚠️ 中国大陆服务器访问 Gemini API 可能被墙。** 先测试连通性：`curl -s -o /dev/null -w '%{http_code}' https://generativelanguage.googleapis.com`，超时则需设置代理或改用海外服务器。
52	
53	## 用法一：概念图（主要用途）
54	
55	概念图没有数据文件输入，靠 `--intent` 描述布局、组件、箭头、颜色语义。
56	
57	```bash
58	# Framework / Pipeline 图
59	ssh_execute("""
60	cd ~/my-project && \
61	GOOGLE_API_KEY=[REDACTED] paperbanana plot \
62	  --intent "Three-stage pipeline diagram flowing left to right:
63	Stage 1 'Data Collection' (blue rounded box) with icons for web scraping and API,
64	Stage 2 'Processing' (green rounded box) with sub-steps filter/transform/validate stacked vertically,
65	Stage 3 'Model Training' (orange rounded box) with GPU icon.
66	Connect stages with thick arrows. Add a feedback loop arrow from Stage 3 back to Stage 1 (dashed, gray).
67	Below the pipeline, a horizontal bar showing 'Time: 2h → 6h → 24h'.
68	White background, no border, publication quality 300 DPI." \
69	  --iterations 3
70	""")
71	
72	# 架构图（多组件 + 交互）
73	ssh_execute("""
74	cd ~/my-project && \
75	GOOGLE_API_KEY=[REDACTED] paperbanana plot \
76	  --intent "System architecture diagram:
77	Top layer: 'User Interface' (light blue rectangle, full width).
78	Middle layer: three equal boxes side by side — 'Auth Service', 'API Gateway', 'Cache' (all light green).
79	Bottom layer: two boxes — 'Database' (left, wider) and 'Message Queue' (right, narrower).
80	Vertical arrows connecting layers. Horizontal dashed arrow between Database and Message Queue.
81	Each box has a small icon in top-left corner. Rounded corners, drop shadow, white background." \
82	  --iterations 3
83	""")
84	```
85	
86	### 概念图意图描述技巧
87	
88	- **明确空间布局**：left-to-right / top-to-bottom / grid，不要让 LLM 猜
89	- **指定组件形状**：rounded box / circle / diamond / hexagon
90	- **颜色绑定语义**：和论文其他图一致（如 baseline=blue, treatment=orange）
91	- **箭头类型**：solid / dashed / thick / thin / bidirectional
92	- **标注所有文字**：每个 box 的 label、箭头上的 annotation
93	
94	## 用法二：探索性统计图
95	
96	已有模板的统计图（heatmap、scatter、bar）优先用 `draw-paper-figure`。PaperBanana 适合新图类型或不确定 layout 的探索。
97	
98	```bash
99	# CSV 数据 + 意图描述
100	ssh_execute("""
101	cd ~/my-project && \
102	GOOGLE_API_KEY=[REDACTED] paperbanana plot \
103	  --data results/delta_ece_summary.csv \
104	  --intent "Diverging heatmap showing ΔECE (quantized minus FP16) for 4 PTQ methods across 6 datasets and 5 models, red=calibration degraded blue=improved, annotate significant cells with asterisk" \
105	  --iterations 3
106	""")
107	
108	# JSON 数据
109	ssh_execute("""
110	cd ~/my-project && \
111	GOOGLE_API_KEY=[REDACTED] paperbanana plot \
112	  --data results/tstar_by_dataset.json \
113	  --intent "Line plot showing T* values across 6 datasets for 5 LLM models, highlight three regime zones with background shading"
114	""")
115	```
116	
117	## 数据格式准备（统计图）
118	
119	PaperBanana 接受 CSV（列名清晰）或 JSON（字典格式）：
120	
121	```python
122	prep_script = """
123	import csv
124	data = [
125	    {'model': 'LLaMA-3.1-8B', 'method': 'GPTQ', 'dataset': 'ARC-C', 'delta_ece': -18.7, 'sig': True},
126	    {'model': 'LLaMA-3.1-8B', 'method': 'AWQ',  'dataset': 'TruthfulQA', 'delta_ece': +17.6, 'sig': False},
127	    # ... 完整数据
128	]
129	with open('results/summary.csv', 'w', newline='') as f:
130	    writer = csv.DictWriter(f, fieldnames=['model','method','dataset','delta_ece','sig'])
131	    writer.writeheader()
132	    writer.writerows(data)
133	print("CSV ready")
134	"""
135	ssh_execute(f"cd ~/my-project && python3 -c '{prep_script}'")
136	```
137	
138	## 输出处理
139	
140	```bash
141	# 找最新生成的图
142	ssh_execute("""
143	latest=$(ls -td ~/my-project/outputs/run_*/ | head -1)
144	echo "Latest: $latest"
145	ls "$latest"
146	""")
147	
148	# 复制到 figures/paper/ 并转 PDF
149	ssh_execute("""
150	latest=$(ls -td ~/my-project/outputs/run_*/ | head -1)
151	cp "$latest"/*.png ~/my-project/figures/paper/
152	cd ~/my-project/figures/paper/
153	for f in *.png; do
154	    python3 -c "
155	import matplotlib
156	matplotlib.use('Agg')
157	import matplotlib.pyplot as plt
158	import matplotlib.image as mpimg
159	img = mpimg.imread('$f')
160	h, w = img.shape[:2]
161	fig = plt.figure(figsize=(w/300, h/300), dpi=300)
162	ax = fig.add_axes([0,0,1,1])
163	ax.imshow(img); ax.axis('off')
164	plt.savefig('${f%.png}.pdf', bbox_inches='tight', pad_inches=0)
165	plt.close()
166	print('PDF:', '${f%.png}.pdf')
167	"
168	done
169	""")
170	```
171	
172	## 迭代优化
173	
174	```bash
175	# 查看 Critic 的评估反馈
176	ssh_execute("""
177	latest=$(ls -td ~/my-project/outputs/run_*/ | head -1)
178	cat "$latest/metadata.json" | python3 -m json.tool | grep -A5 "critic"
179	""")
180	
181	# 重跑时加强意图描述（加更多约束）
182	ssh_execute("""
183	GOOGLE_API_KEY=[REDACTED] paperbanana plot \
184	  --intent "... [更具体的空间布局 / 颜色语义 / 组件关系描述] ..." \
185	  --iterations 5
186	""")
187	```
188	
189	## 注意事项
190	
191	- 每张图大约消耗 3-5 次 Gemini API 调用（3 轮迭代），Gemini free tier 够用
192	- 生成时间 1-3 分钟/图（含 LLM 调用 + 本地执行）
193	- 如果服务器无法访问 Gemini，改用 `draw-paper-figure`（完全离线）
194	- 概念图的 intent 描述越详细越好——空间布局、组件关系、颜色语义都要写清楚
195	
````

</details>

      "content": "---\ndescription: 用 PaperBanana 生成学术图表——概念图（架构/流程/framework）首选，探索性统计图也可。LLM 写 matplotlib 代码 + VLM Critic 闭环迭代。需要 GOOGLE_API_KEY。\nname: paper-banana\nscope: global\ntags:\n  - visualization\n  - paper\n  - matplotlib\n  - llm-codegen\n  - conceptual-diagram\nversion: 2\ntools:\n  - ssh_execute\n---\n\n# Paper Banana — LLM 驱动的学术图表生成\n\nPaperBanana 由 Gemini 自动写 matplotlib 代码，VLM Critic 评估渲染结果并迭代优化（默认 3 轮），输出 NeurIPS 风格 300 DPI 图表。\n\n## 适用范围（与 paper-visualization.skill.md L4 路由一致）\n\n| 场景 | 推荐工具 | 原因 |\n|------|---------|------|\n| **概念图**（架构图、流程图、framework 示意） | **PaperBanana** | matplotlib 手写 boxes/arrows/icons 极痛苦，LLM 生成代码更灵活 |\n| **统计图**（scatter、bar、line、heatmap、forest） | **matplotlib 手写** | 确定性、可控、有模板 |\n| **探索性图**，不确定 layout | **PaperBanana** | LLM 多轮迭代试错 |\n| 离线 / 无 GOOGLE_API_KEY | **matplotlib 手写** | 不依赖外部 API |\n\n> **核心定位**：PaperBanana 的真正优势是**概念图**——这类图没有数据输入、是纯视觉布局，手写 matplotlib 代码极其繁琐。统计图有成熟模板（见 `draw-paper-figure.skill.md`），手写更可控。\n>\n> **与 draw-paper-figure 的区别**：draw-paper-figure 是手写模板（快、可控、确定性）；paper-banana 是 LLM 自动生成代码（慢、灵活、适合新图类型）。\n\n## 前置条件：GOOGLE_API_KEY\n\n```bash\n# 检查 key 是否已配置\nssh_execute(\"echo $GOOGLE_API_KEY | head -c 10\")\n\n# 如未配置，需要用户提供 key，然后：\nssh_execute(\"echo 'export GOOGLE_API_KEY=[REDACTED]' >> ~/.bashrc && source ~/.bashrc\")\n\n# 安装 PaperBanana\nssh_execute(\"pip install paperbanana -q\")\n\n# 验证安装\nssh_execute(\"paperbanana --version\")\n```\n\n**⚠️ 如果 GOOGLE_API_KEY 未设置，直接停下来问用户要 key，不要尝试绕过。**\n\n**⚠️ 中国大陆服务器访问 Gemini API 可能被墙。** 先测试连通性：`curl -s -o /dev/null -w '%{http_code}' https://generativelanguage.googleapis.com`，超时则需设置代理或改用海外服务器。\n\n## 用法一：概念图（主要用途）\n\n概念图没有数据文件输入，靠 `--intent` 描述布局、组件、箭头、颜色语义。\n\n```bash\n# Framework / Pipeline 图\nssh_execute(\"\"\"\ncd ~/my-project && \\\nGOOGLE_API_KEY=[REDACTED] paperbanana plot \\\n  --intent \"Three-stage pipeline diagram flowing left to right:\nStage 1 'Data Collection' (blue rounded box) with icons for web scraping and API,\nStage 2 'Processing' (green rounded box) with sub-steps filter/transform/validate stacked vertically,\nStage 3 'Model Training' (orange rounded box) with GPU icon.\nConnect stages with thick arrows. Add a feedback loop arrow from Stage 3 back to Stage 1 (dashed, gray).\nBelow the pipeline, a horizontal bar showing 'Time: 2h → 6h → 24h'.\nWhite background, no border, publication quality 300 DPI.\" \\\n  --iterations 3\n\"\"\")\n\n# 架构图（多组件 + 交互）\nssh_execute(\"\"\"\ncd ~/my-project && \\\nGOOGLE_API_KEY=[REDACTED] paperbanana plot \\\n  --intent \"System architecture diagram:\nTop layer: 'User Interface' (light blue rectangle, full width).\nMiddle layer: three equal boxes side by side — 'Auth Service', 'API Gateway', 'Cache' (all light green).\nBottom layer: two boxes — 'Database' (left, wider) and 'Message Queue' (right, narrower).\nVertical arrows connecting layers. Horizontal dashed arrow between Database and Message Queue.\nEach box has a small icon in top-left corner. Rounded corners, drop shadow, white background.\" \\\n  --iterations 3\n\"\"\")\n```\n\n### 概念图意图描述技巧\n\n- **明确空间布局**：left-to-right / top-to-bottom / grid，不要让 LLM 猜\n- **指定组件形状**：rounded box / circle / diamond / hexagon\n- **颜色绑定语义**：和论文其他图一致（如 baseline=blue, treatment=orange）\n- **箭头类型**：solid / dashed / thick / thin / bidirectional\n- **标注所有文字**：每个 box 的 label、箭头上的 annotation\n\n## 用法二：探索性统计图\n\n已有模板的统计图（heatmap、scatter、bar）优先用 `draw-paper-figure`。PaperBanana 适合新图类型或不确定 layout 的探索。\n\n```bash\n# CSV 数据 + 意图描述\nssh_execute(\"\"\"\ncd ~/my-project && \\\nGOOGLE_API_KEY=[REDACTED] paperbanana plot \\\n  --data results/delta_ece_summary.csv \\\n  --intent \"Diverging heatmap showing ΔECE (quantized minus FP16) for 4 PTQ methods across 6 datasets and 5 models, red=calibration degraded blue=improved, annotate significant cells with asterisk\" \\\n  --iterations 3\n\"\"\")\n\n# JSON 数据\nssh_execute(\"\"\"\ncd ~/my-project && \\\nGOOGLE_API_KEY=[REDACTED] paperbanana plot \\\n  --data results/tstar_by_dataset.json \\\n  --intent \"Line plot showing T* values across 6 datasets for 5 LLM models, highlight three regime zones with background shading\"\n\"\"\")\n```\n\n## 数据格式准备（统计图）\n\nPaperBanana 接受 CSV（列名清晰）或 JSON（字典格式）：\n\n```python\nprep_script = \"\"\"\nimport csv\ndata = [\n    {'model': 'LLaMA-3.1-8B', 'method': 'GPTQ', 'dataset': 'ARC-C', 'delta_ece': -18.7, 'sig': True},\n    {'model': 'LLaMA-3.1-8B', 'method': 'AWQ',  'dataset': 'TruthfulQA', 'delta_ece': +17.6, 'sig': False},\n    # ... 完整数据\n]\nwith open('results/summary.csv', 'w', newline='') as f:\n    writer = csv.DictWriter(f, fieldnames=['model','method','dataset','delta_ece','sig'])\n    writer.writeheader()\n    writer.writerows(data)\nprint(\"CSV ready\")\n\"\"\"\nssh_execute(f\"cd ~/my-project && python3 -c '{prep_script}'\")\n```\n\n## 输出处理\n\n```bash\n# 找最新生成的图\nssh_execute(\"\"\"\nlatest=$(ls -td ~/my-project/outputs/run_*/ | head -1)\necho \"Latest: $latest\"\nls \"$latest\"\n\"\"\")\n\n# 复制到 figures/paper/ 并转 PDF\nssh_execute(\"\"\"\nlatest=$(ls -td ~/my-project/outputs/run_*/ | head -1)\ncp \"$latest\"/*.png ~/my-project/figures/paper/\ncd ~/my-project/figures/paper/\nfor f in *.png; do\n    python3 -c \"\nimport matplotlib\nmatplotlib.use('Agg')\nimport matplotlib.pyplot as plt\nimport matplotlib.image as mpimg\nimg = mpimg.imread('$f')\nh, w = img.shape[:2]\nfig = plt.figure(figsize=(w/300, h/300), dpi=300)\nax = fig.add_axes([0,0,1,1])\nax.imshow(img); ax.axis('off')\nplt.savefig('${f%.png}.pdf', bbox_inches='tight', pad_inches=0)\nplt.close()\nprint('PDF:', '${f%.png}.pdf')\n\"\ndone\n\"\"\")\n```\n\n## 迭代优化\n\n```bash\n# 查看 Critic 的评估反馈\nssh_execute(\"\"\"\nlatest=$(ls -td ~/my-project/outputs/run_*/ | head -1)\ncat \"$latest/metadata.json\" | python3 -m json.tool | grep -A5 \"critic\"\n\"\"\")\n\n# 重跑时加强意图描述（加更多约束）\nssh_execute(\"\"\"\nGOOGLE_API_KEY=[REDACTED] paperbanana plot \\\n  --intent \"... [更具体的空间布局 / 颜色语义 / 组件关系描述] ...\" \\\n  --iterations 5\n\"\"\")\n```\n\n## 注意事项\n\n- 每张图大约消耗 3-5 次 Gemini API 调用（3 轮迭代），Gemini free tier 够用\n- 生成时间 1-3 分钟/图（含 LLM 调用 + 本地执行）\n- 如果服务器无法访问 Gemini，改用 `draw-paper-figure`（完全离线）\n- 概念图的 intent 描述越详细越好——空间布局、组件关系、颜色语义都要写清楚\n",
      "numLines": 195,
      "startLine": 1,
      "totalLines": 195
    }
  },

       \begin{figure}[H]
       \centering
       \includegraphics[width=\columnwidth]{fig1_differential_response.pdf}
       \caption{Differential depth response (DDR). Diamonds: cross-generator means; dots: per-generator values. Ref.\\ NLL & Maximum token surprise \\\\\n\\texttt{entropy\\_min}  & Ref.\\ NLL & Minimum token surprise \\\\\n\\texttt{tail\\_decay}   & Ref.\\ NLL & Log-prob decay slope (ranks 100--1K) \\\\\n\\midrule\n\\texttt{ttr}           & Surface & Type-token ratio \\\\\n\\texttt{hapax\\_ratio}  & Surface & Proportion of hapax legomena \\\\\n\\bottomrule\n\\end{tabular}\n\\end{table}\n\n\\subsection{Task Formulation}\n\\label{sec:task}\n\nGiven a text $x$, we predict its \\emph{generation depth} $d \\in \\{0, 1, 2{+}\\}$: human-written ($d{=}0$), first-generation LLM output ($d{=}1$), or text produced by two or more recursive LLM generations ($d{=}2{+}$).\nA generation chain of depth $k$ is defined recursively: $x^{(0)} \\sim \\mathcal{C}_0$ (human corpus), $x^{(k)} = G(x^{(k-1)})$ for $k \\geq 1$, where $G$ denotes stochastic autoregressive generation.\nDepths beyond 2 are merged into $2{+}$ because feature convergence compresses inter-class margins at deeper levels (\\S\\ref{sec:results-differential}).\n\n\\subsection{Generation Chain Construction}\n\\label{sec:generation}\n\nWe construct generation chains using eight generators spanning five model families: \\textbf{Pythia} (2.8B, 1B) \\cite{pythia}, \\textbf{OLMo} (OLMo-2-1B) \\cite{olmo}, \\textbf{Qwen} (Qwen3-1.7B, Qwen3-0.6B, Qwen3-8B-Base) \\cite{qwen3}, \\textbf{Llama} (Llama-3.2-3B) \\cite{llama3}, and \\textbf{Mistral} (Mistral-7B-v0.3) \\cite{mistral7b}.\nThese generators span five distinct architectures with a 13$\\times$ parameter range (0.6B--8B), trained on heterogeneous corpora; Qwen contributes three models (0.6B--8B) covering the full scale range; Qwen's overrepresentation (3/8 generators) is a limitation, but LOFO holding out all three Qwen models (F1\\,=\\,0.686) provides the strictest family-level test.\n\nFor each generator, we sample approximately 1{,}000 seed passages from English Wikipedia as $\\mathcal{C}_0$.\nThe same pool of seed passages serves as gen-0 for all generators.\nWe use shared seeds across generators so that cross-generator comparisons control for input content and isolate the effect of the generation model from source text variation.\nIn LOGO evaluation, gen-0 samples from the held-out generator's chains also appear (with the same text) in the training generators' chains, creating a potential train--test overlap for the gen-0 class.\nWe address this with a supplementary binary evaluation excluding gen-0 entirely (\\S\\ref{sec:results-baselines}).\nEach seed is iteratively fed through the generator to produce a chain of depth 4 (i.e., gen-0 through gen-4), using the prompt ``\\texttt{Continue the following text: \\{text\\}}''.\nWe generate up to depth~4 to empirically confirm convergence: 5-class analysis shows adjacent depths 2--4 become indistinguishable (F1\\,$\\approx$\\,0.27; Appendix~\\ref{app:finer-depth}), motivating the gen-2+ class collapse.\nAll generation uses temperature $\\tau{=}0.7$, nucleus sampling with $p{=}0.9$, and maximum length of 256 new tokens.\nAfter filtering chain breaks and passages too short for feature extraction, 37{,}058 texts remain across all depths (Appendix~\\ref{app:dataset-stats}); depths 2--4 are collapsed into $2{+}$.\n\n\\subsection{Feature Extraction}\n\\label{sec:features}\n\n\\paragraph{Terminology.} Throughout this paper, we refer to per-token cross-entropy loss under a fixed reference model as ``entropy'' for conciseness; these are reference-model NLL values, not true information-theoretic entropy of the generating distribution.\n\nWe deliberately use interpretable distributional statistics so that the DDR partition remains transparent; end-to-end neural features improve classification ($+$11.6\\,pp; Appendix~\\ref{app:qlora}) but obscure the decomposition.\nLeave-one-feature-out analysis (Appendix~\\ref{app:lofo}) confirms that both reference-model and surface feature groups contribute independently.\n\nWe extract seven distributional features per text (Table~\\ref{tab:features}), selected to provide principled coverage of the NLL distribution---location (mean), spread (std), extremes (max, min), and tail structure (decay)---complemented by two reference-free surface diversity measures.\nWe evaluated six additional NLL statistics (median, 90th percentile, IQR, CV, skewness, kurtosis) as candidate features; all exhibit $|r| > 0.65$ with existing features and yield no classification gain when added ($\\Delta$F1\\,$\\leq$\\,$-0.011$; Appendix~\\ref{app:feature-candidate}).\n\n\\paragraph{Reference-model features.}\nA held-out reference model (Pythia-410M, chosen to be smaller than all generators) computes per-token negative log-likelihood (NLL) for each text.\nAlthough Pythia-410M shares the GPT-NeoX architecture with two of the eight generators, cross-family reference validation (Appendix~\\ref{app:cross-ref}) shows F1 spread of only 0.013 across Pythia, OLMo, and Qwen references, confirming DDR is reference-model agnostic; however, all tested references are causal LMs.\nFrom the resulting NLL sequence, we extract five statistics: mean, standard deviation, maximum, and minimum of the token-level NLL, plus tail\\_decay---the slope of the vocabulary-level averaged log-probability distribution in ranks 100--1000, capturing the power-law regime where depth-induced compression is most detectable while avoiding high-frequency head noise and sparse extreme-tail counts.\n\n\\paragraph{Surface features.}\nTwo features capture lexical diversity without requiring a reference model: type-token ratio (TTR) and the proportion of words appearing exactly once (hapax\\_ratio).\n\n\\paragraph{Feature complementarity.}\nThese features exhibit a \\emph{differential depth response} (DDR), separating into drifting and non-drifting groups (\\S\\ref{sec:results-differential}; pairwise correlations in Appendix~\\ref{sec:appendix-feature-correlation}).\nTTR and hapax ratio are strongly correlated ($r = 0.97$); both are retained for marginal LOFO gains.\n\n\\subsection{Evaluation Protocol}\n\\label{sec:evaluation}\n\n\\paragraph{Cross-validation.}\nWe evaluate generalization via \\textbf{Leave-One-Generator-Out (LOGO)} cross-validation: each fold holds out one generator entirely, so the classifier must generalize to a generator absent from training---the strictest per-model protocol for assessing deployment against previously unseen generators.\nLOGO does not isolate family-level effects; \\textbf{Leave-One-Family-Out (LOFO)} addresses this by holding out entire model families (binary F1\\,=\\,0.686; \\S\\ref{sec:results-classification}).\nGenerator subsets vary across experiments (Appendix~\\ref{app:gen-subsets-section}).\n\n\\paragraph{Classifier.}\nWe use XGBoost \\cite{xgboost} throughout ($n\\_\\text{estimators}{=}200$, $\\max\\_\\text{depth}{=}6$, learning rate $0.1$; fixed without tuning), with identical hyperparameters across all methods in Table~\\ref{tab:baselines}.\nAlternative classifiers (CORAL \\cite{coral_ordinal}, CORN \\cite{corn_ordinal}, Frank-Hall \\cite{frank_hall_ordinal}) yield comparable performance (Appendix~\\ref{app:classifier}); discriminative power resides in the features, not the classifier.\n\n\\paragraph{Metrics and statistical testing.}\nThe primary metric is \\textbf{macro-averaged F1} over the three depth classes, with 95\\% bootstrap CIs ($n{=}1{,}000$).\nAll $p$-values are two-sided with Bonferroni correction within each family of simultaneous tests.\n\n\\paragraph{Baselines.}\nWe compare the 7-feature ensemble against four single-score detection methods repurposed as depth predictors: Avg-LogProb, FastDetectGPT \\cite{fast_detectgpt}, Binoculars \\cite{binoculars}, and LD-Score \\cite{ld_score}.\nEach baseline score is used as a univariate feature in the same XGBoost framework under LOGO cross-validation to ensure a controlled comparison.\n\n\\paragraph{Length confound control.}\nSince TTR and hapax\\_ratio are mechanically length-dependent, we verify depth signal is not a length artifact via length-matched subsampling that equalizes length distributions across depth classes (\\S\\ref{sec:results-classification}; Appendix~\\ref{app:length}).\n",
        "numLines": 95,
        "startLine": 1,
        "totalLines": 95
      }
    },

  },

    "content": {
      "type": "text",
      "file": {
        "filePath": "/home/ubuntu/.agent-ml-research-idea_gen_0514_3/projects/gen-depth-fingerprint/docs/paper/discussion.tex",
        "content": "\\vspace{-3mm}\n\\section{Discussion}\n\\label{sec:discussion}\n\\vspace{-2mm}\n\n\\looseness=-1 We hypothesize that DDR reflects an empirical regularity of base-model autoregressive generation: recursive inputs compress the token-level NLL distribution monotonically (Appendix~\\ref{app:nll-distribution}), producing location drift independent of the token-selection mechanism.\nControlled ablations support this: greedy decoding preserves the full partition (Appendix~\\ref{app:decoding-strategy}), temperature modulates drift magnitude while preserving the partition (Appendix~\\ref{app:sampling-sensitivity}), and semantic-constraint attenuation confirms predictions (Appendix~\\ref{app:prompt-type}).\nANOVA quantifies the asymmetry: $\\eta^2_{\\text{depth}}{=}0.34$--$0.37$ for location vs.\\ ${<}0.01$ for shape (Appendix~\\ref{app:variance-decomposition}).\n\n\\looseness=-1 Depth constitutes a new analytical dimension for corpus auditing; batch contamination estimation reaches ${\\pm}3$--$4$\\% at $n{=}1{,}000$ (Appendix~\\ref{app:error-analysis}).\nDDR is mechanistically distinct from model collapse \\cite{curse_of_recursion,strong_model_collapse}---retraining erodes tail via weight updates, whereas inference-chain drift shifts only location---and complements concurrent iterative-generation studies (Appendix~\\ref{app:iterative-comparison}).\nInstruction tuning compresses DDR (QLoRA: F1\\,=\\,0.89--0.91; Appendix~\\ref{app:instruct}); cross-domain signal persists (F1\\,=\\,0.74--0.75; Appendix~\\ref{app:domain}).\n\\vspace{-2mm}\n",
        "numLines": 14,
        "startLine": 1,
        "totalLines": 14
      }
    },

  },

{
  "parentUuid": "d850bbbe-93f5-4d43-aaff-64eb52cc4dcf",
  "isSidechain": false,
  "attachment": {
    "type": "file",
    "filename": "/home/ubuntu/.agent-ml-research-idea_gen_0514_3/projects/gen-depth-fingerprint/docs/paper/introduction.tex",
    "content": {
      "type": "text",
      "file": {
        "filePath": "/home/ubuntu/.agent-ml-research-idea_gen_0514_3/projects/gen-depth-fingerprint/docs/paper/introduction.tex",
        "content": "\\section{Introduction}\n\\label{sec:introduction}\n\nAn estimated 7--17\\% of recent scientific publications already contain substantial LLM-modified content \\cite{mapping_llm_science,monitoring_ai_review}. As these outputs re-enter training corpora \\cite{dolma,fineweb} and seed further generation, text accumulates at varying \\emph{generation depths}; each successive cycle reshapes distributional properties in ways that matter for data provenance, corpus auditing, and contamination monitoring in base-model pretraining corpora \\cite{ccnet,dsir} as well as model collapse analysis \\cite{curse_of_recursion,strong_model_collapse}. Understanding \\emph{how} recursive generation alters text distributions---and which properties change versus which are preserved---is therefore a prerequisite for principled corpus curation, yet current methods address only binary human-or-machine detection \\cite{detectgpt,fast_detectgpt,binoculars,ld_score}.\nBase models provide the cleanest substrate to isolate depth effects from alignment-induced changes.\nWe find that recursive generation produces a decomposable depth profile: distributional \\emph{location} drifts with depth while \\emph{shape} remains stable across generators, an asymmetry sufficient for cross-generator depth prediction.\n\nDistributional compression under recursive generation is well-documented \\cite{curious_decline,broken_telephone,markovian_chains}, yet existing analyses characterize \\emph{that} and \\emph{why} convergence occurs without decomposing what drifts from what is preserved.\nWe introduce \\emph{depth profiling} (Figure~\\ref{fig:framework}), classifying text by ordinal generation depth (gen-0\\,/\\,gen-1\\,/\\,gen-2+), and decompose this compression into structurally distinct components: across eight base-model generators spanning five architectural families (0.6B--8B), the NLL distribution's central tendency (mean entropy (reference-model NLL), lexical diversity) drifts monotonically with depth while its shape (entropy extremes, tail structure) remains an order of magnitude more stable ($d{=}0.76$--$1.17$ vs.\\ $d < 0.10$ for three near-stationary features; entropy std is intermediate ($d{=}0.55$); boundary features show additional nuance; convergent effect-size measures confirm this partition).\nWe term this location/shape asymmetry the \\emph{differential depth response} (DDR; Figure~\\ref{fig:ddr-trajectory}), an empirical regularity of base-model autoregressive generation that is mechanistically distinct from model collapse \\cite{curse_of_recursion}, which erodes tail coverage via weight updates; DDR instead analyzes fixed-parameter inference chains.\nThe stationary shape component provides the cross-generator reference needed for depth prediction without per-generator calibration.\n\n\\begin{figure*}[t!]\n\\centering\n\\includegraphics[width=\\textwidth]{fig2_framework.pdf}\n\\caption{Depth profiling framework. (a)~Recursive generation chain: a human-written seed (gen-0) is iteratively fed through a generator $G$ to produce text at successive depths; eight base-model LLMs across five families (0.6B--8B) serve as generators. (b)~Feature extraction: each text is scored by a fixed reference model (Pythia-410M), yielding five NLL statistics and two surface diversity measures (seven DDR features total). (c)~Leave-One-Generator-Out evaluation: XGBoost classifies each text into gen-0\\,/\\,gen-1\\,/\\,gen-2+ with one generator held out per fold.}\n\\label{fig:framework}\n\\end{figure*}\nWe focus on base (pretrained) models; instruction tuning degrades depth signal (Appendix~\\ref{app:instruct}).\n\nAs a proof of concept, a lightweight 7-feature ensemble informed by DDR achieves leak-free binary F1\\,=\\,0.694 under leave-one-generator-out evaluation, matching multi-detector ensembles at ${\\sim}2.4\\times$ lower cost; an end-to-end QLoRA classifier reaches 3-class F1\\,=\\,0.905 (6-gen LOGO; Appendix~\\ref{app:qlora}), confirming that the depth signal extends well beyond handcrafted features.\n\nOur contributions:\n\\begin{enumerate}[nosep,leftmargin=*]\n    \\item \\textbf{Empirical decomposition (DDR).} Across eight generators from five families, we decompose inference-chain drift into a location component that shifts monotonically ($d{=}0.76$--$1.17$) and a shape component that remains near-stationary ($d < 0.10$ for three near-stationary features). Where prior work documents \\emph{what converges} \\cite{curious_decline,markovian_chains}, DDR identifies \\emph{what is approximately preserved} and enables depth detection without per-generator calibration. DDR is a descriptive empirical finding; the classification value lies in this decomposition rather than the classifier itself.\n    \\item \\textbf{Task formulation and evaluation suite.} We define generation depth classification as an ordinal prediction task and construct a controlled dataset of 37K texts from eight generators across five families (0.6B--8B), with leave-one-generator-out cross-validation as the primary evaluation protocol.\n    \\item \\textbf{Scope characterization.} Systematic evaluation across temperature, prompt type, instruction tuning, and model scale maps the operational envelope of depth profiling, identifying key challenges in instruction-tuned models, cross-domain transfer, and perturbation robustness.\n\\end{enumerate}\n",
        "numLines": 29,
        "startLine": 1,
        "totalLines": 29
      }
    },
    "displayPath": "../../../docs/paper/introduction.tex"
  },

````text
1	---
2	description: 用 PaperBanana 生成学术图表——概念图（架构/流程/framework）首选，探索性统计图也可。LLM 写 matplotlib 代码 + VLM Critic 闭环迭代。需要 GOOGLE_API_KEY。

14	---
15	
16	# Paper Banana — LLM 驱动的学术图表生成
17	
18	PaperBanana 由 Gemini 自动写 matplotlib 代码，VLM Critic 评估渲染结果并迭代优化（默认 3 轮），输出 NeurIPS 风格 300 DPI 图表。
19	
20	## 适用范围（与 paper-visualization.skill.md L4 路由一致）
21	
22	| 场景 | 推荐工具 | 原因 |
23	|------|---------|------|
24	| **概念图**（架构图、流程图、framework 示意） | **PaperBanana** | matplotlib 手写 boxes/arrows/icons 极痛苦，LLM 生成代码更灵活 |
25	| **统计图**（scatter、bar、line、heatmap、forest） | **matplotlib 手写** | 确定性、可控、有模板 |
26	| **探索性图**，不确定 layout | **PaperBanana** | LLM 多轮迭代试错 |
27	| 离线 / 无 GOOGLE_API_KEY | **matplotlib 手写** | 不依赖外部 API |
28	
29	> **核心定位**：PaperBanana 的真正优势是**概念图**——这类图没有数据输入、是纯视觉布局，手写 matplotlib 代码极其繁琐。统计图有成熟模板（见 `draw-paper-figure.skill.md`），手写更可控。
30	>
31	> **与 draw-paper-figure 的区别**：draw-paper-figure 是手写模板（快、可控、确定性）；paper-banana 是 LLM 自动生成代码（慢、灵活、适合新图类型）。
32	
33	## 前置条件：GOOGLE_API_KEY
34	
35	```bash
36	# 检查 key 是否已配置
37	ssh_execute("echo $GOOGLE_API_KEY | head -c 10")
38	
39	# 如未配置，需要用户提供 key，然后：
40	ssh_execute("echo 'export GOOGLE_API_KEY=[REDACTED]' >> ~/.bashrc && source ~/.bashrc")
41	
42	# 安装 PaperBanana
43	ssh_execute("pip install paperbanana -q")
44	
45	# 验证安装
46	ssh_execute("paperbanana --version")
47	```
48	
49	**⚠️ 如果 GOOGLE_API_KEY 未设置，直接停下来问用户要 key，不要尝试绕过。**
50	
51	**⚠️ 中国大陆服务器访问 Gemini API 可能被墙。** 先测试连通性：`curl -s -o /dev/null -w '%{http_code}' https://generativelanguage.googleapis.com`，超时则需设置代理或改用海外服务器。
52	
53	## 用法一：概念图（主要用途）
54	
55	概念图没有数据文件输入，靠 `--intent` 描述布局、组件、箭头、颜色语义。
56	
57	```bash
58	# Framework / Pipeline 图
59	ssh_execute("""
60	cd ~/my-project && \
61	GOOGLE_API_KEY=[REDACTED] paperbanana plot \
62	  --intent "Three-stage pipeline diagram flowing left to right:
63	Stage 1 'Data Collection' (blue rounded box) with icons for web scraping and API,
64	Stage 2 'Processing' (green rounded box) with sub-steps filter/transform/validate stacked vertically,
65	Stage 3 'Model Training' (orange rounded box) with GPU icon.
66	Connect stages with thick arrows. Add a feedback loop arrow from Stage 3 back to Stage 1 (dashed, gray).
67	Below the pipeline, a horizontal bar showing 'Time: 2h → 6h → 24h'.
68	White background, no border, publication quality 300 DPI." \
69	  --iterations 3
70	""")
71	
72	# 架构图（多组件 + 交互）
73	ssh_execute("""
74	cd ~/my-project && \
75	GOOGLE_API_KEY=[REDACTED] paperbanana plot \
76	  --intent "System architecture diagram:
77	Top layer: 'User Interface' (light blue rectangle, full width).
78	Middle layer: three equal boxes side by side — 'Auth Service', 'API Gateway', 'Cache' (all light green).
79	Bottom layer: two boxes — 'Database' (left, wider) and 'Message Queue' (right, narrower).
80	Vertical arrows connecting layers. Horizontal dashed arrow between Database and Message Queue.
81	Each box has a small icon in top-left corner. Rounded corners, drop shadow, white background." \
82	  --iterations 3
83	""")
84	```
85	
86	### 概念图意图描述技巧
87	
88	- **明确空间布局**：left-to-right / top-to-bottom / grid，不要让 LLM 猜
89	- **指定组件形状**：rounded box / circle / diamond / hexagon
90	- **颜色绑定语义**：和论文其他图一致（如 baseline=blue, treatment=orange）
91	- **箭头类型**：solid / dashed / thick / thin / bidirectional
92	- **标注所有文字**：每个 box 的 label、箭头上的 annotation
93	
94	## 用法二：探索性统计图
95	
96	已有模板的统计图（heatmap、scatter、bar）优先用 `draw-paper-figure`。PaperBanana 适合新图类型或不确定 layout 的探索。
97	
98	```bash
99	# CSV 数据 + 意图描述
100	ssh_execute("""
101	cd ~/my-project && \
102	GOOGLE_API_KEY=[REDACTED] paperbanana plot \
103	  --data results/delta_ece_summary.csv \
104	  --intent "Diverging heatmap showing ΔECE (quantized minus FP16) for 4 PTQ methods across 6 datasets and 5 models, red=calibration degraded blue=improved, annotate significant cells with asterisk" \
105	  --iterations 3
106	""")
107	
108	# JSON 数据
109	ssh_execute("""
110	cd ~/my-project && \
111	GOOGLE_API_KEY=[REDACTED] paperbanana plot \
112	  --data results/tstar_by_dataset.json \
113	  --intent "Line plot showing T* values across 6 datasets for 5 LLM models, highlight three regime zones with background shading"
114	""")
115	```
116	
117	## 数据格式准备（统计图）
118	
119	PaperBanana 接受 CSV（列名清晰）或 JSON（字典格式）：
120	
121	```python
122	prep_script = """
123	import csv
124	data = [
125	    {'model': 'LLaMA-3.1-8B', 'method': 'GPTQ', 'dataset': 'ARC-C', 'delta_ece': -18.7, 'sig': True},
126	    {'model': 'LLaMA-3.1-8B', 'method': 'AWQ',  'dataset': 'TruthfulQA', 'delta_ece': +17.6, 'sig': False},
127	    # ... 完整数据
128	]
129	with open('results/summary.csv', 'w', newline='') as f:
130	    writer = csv.DictWriter(f, fieldnames=['model','method','dataset','delta_ece','sig'])
131	    writer.writeheader()
132	    writer.writerows(data)
133	print("CSV ready")
134	"""
135	ssh_execute(f"cd ~/my-project && python3 -c '{prep_script}'")
136	```
137	
138	## 输出处理
139	
140	```bash
141	# 找最新生成的图
142	ssh_execute("""
143	latest=$(ls -td ~/my-project/outputs/run_*/ | head -1)
144	echo "Latest: $latest"
145	ls "$latest"
146	""")
147	
148	# 复制到 figures/paper/ 并转 PDF
149	ssh_execute("""
150	latest=$(ls -td ~/my-project/outputs/run_*/ | head -1)
151	cp "$latest"/*.png ~/my-project/figures/paper/
152	cd ~/my-project/figures/paper/
153	for f in *.png; do
154	    python3 -c "
155	import matplotlib
156	matplotlib.use('Agg')
157	import matplotlib.pyplot as plt
158	import matplotlib.image as mpimg
159	img = mpimg.imread('$f')
160	h, w = img.shape[:2]
161	fig = plt.figure(figsize=(w/300, h/300), dpi=300)
162	ax = fig.add_axes([0,0,1,1])
163	ax.imshow(img); ax.axis('off')
164	plt.savefig('${f%.png}.pdf', bbox_inches='tight', pad_inches=0)
165	plt.close()
166	print('PDF:', '${f%.png}.pdf')
167	"
168	done
169	""")
170	```
171	
172	## 迭代优化
173	
174	```bash
175	# 查看 Critic 的评估反馈
176	ssh_execute("""
177	latest=$(ls -td ~/my-project/outputs/run_*/ | head -1)
178	cat "$latest/metadata.json" | python3 -m json.tool | grep -A5 "critic"
179	""")
180	
181	# 重跑时加强意图描述（加更多约束）
182	ssh_execute("""
183	GOOGLE_API_KEY=[REDACTED] paperbanana plot \
184	  --intent "... [更具体的空间布局 / 颜色语义 / 组件关系描述] ..." \
185	  --iterations 5
186	""")
187	```
188	
189	## 注意事项
190	
191	- 每张图大约消耗 3-5 次 Gemini API 调用（3 轮迭代），Gemini free tier 够用
192	- 生成时间 1-3 分钟/图（含 LLM 调用 + 本地执行）
193	- 如果服务器无法访问 Gemini，改用 `draw-paper-figure`（完全离线）
194	- 概念图的 intent 描述越详细越好——空间布局、组件关系、颜色语义都要写清楚
195	
````

(faded)\n- Left side: depth labels d=0, d=1, d=2+\n- Color gradient: gen-0 in light blue, gen-1 in medium blue, gen-2+ in dark blue\n- Small formula: x^(k) = G(x^(k-1))\n\nPanel (b) 'Feature Extraction' (middle, ~35% width):\n- Top: input document flowing into a box labeled 'Pythia-410M (Reference Model)'\n- Below: NLL distribution curve sketch (small)\n- Below that: two groups of extracted features in rounded boxes:\n  Group 1 'NLL Statistics' (5 items stacked): entropy_mean, entropy_std, entropy_max, entropy_min, tail_decay\n  Group 2 'Surface Features' (2 items): TTR, hapax_ratio\n- Bottom: these merge into a single vector labeled '7 DDR Features'\n- Color: green/teal for feature boxes\n\nPanel (c) 'LOGO Evaluation' (rightmost, ~30% width):\n- Top: 2x4 grid of 8 small generator icons (Pythia-2.8B, Pythia-1B, OLMo-2-1B, Qwen3-1.7B, Qwen3-0.6B, Qwen3-8B, Llama-3.2-3B, Mistral-7B), 7 in blue (train), 1 highlighted in orange/red (test/held-out)\n- Arrow down to a box labeled 'XGBoost Classifier'\n- Arrow down to three prediction outputs: gen-0, gen-1, gen-2+ (matching the blue gradient from panel a)\n- Small text: 'Leave-One-Generator-Out'\n\nOverall style: clean, minimal, no heavy borders. Examples: \"rebind ctrl+s\", \"add a chord shortcut\", \"change the submit key\", \"customize keybindings\".\n- simplify: Review changed code for reuse, quality, and efficiency, then fix any issues found.\n- fewer-permission-prompts: Scan your transcripts for common read-only Bash and MCP tool calls, then add a prioritized allowlist to project .claude/settings.json to reduce permission prompts.\n- loop: Run a prompt or slash command on a recurring interval (e.g. /loop 5m /foo). <details><summary>Logged reasoning block</summary>

```text
The PaperBanana-generated figure looks decent but has some issues compared to what I'd expect for a top-venue paper:

1. The layout is clean and has the 3-panel structure
2. The depth gradient (light blue → medium blue → dark blue) is there
3. The generator grid in panel (c) is present with Mistral-7B highlighted in orange
4. The feature extraction panel shows the two groups correctly

However, there are some quality concerns:
- The figure looks a bit sparse/simple for an EMNLP paper
- The arrows and boxes could be more polished
- The NLL Distribution text is just text, no curve sketch


- The overall visual density is moderate

I should share this with the user so they can decide whether to keep it or go back to the original.
```

  "content": "[Reviewer 回复]:\n[severity: warning] Figure 2 PaperBanana 重绘审查报告\n\n## 产出确认\nWorker 名为 \"fig1-paperbanana\" 但实际产出为 **fig2_framework_paperbanana.pdf/png**（17:25-17:31），是 Method 部分的 framework 图而非 Figure 1。\n\n## 内容准确性 ✅\n新图 3 个 panel 信息均正确：\n- (a) Recursive Generation Chain: 8 LLMs, 5 families, 0.6B-8B, 方程 x^(k)=G(x^(k-1))\n- (b) Feature Extraction: Pythia-410M → NLL Distribution → 5 NLL Statistics + 2 Surface → 7 DDR Features\n- (c) LOGO Evaluation: 8 generators 正确列出，Mistral-7B 作为 held-out 示例，XGBoost → 3-class output\n\n视觉质量好，排版干净，信息密度适中。\n\n## WARNING: 图未集成到论文中\n\n当前 method.tex **没有任何 \\includegraphics 或 \\begin{figure}**。新图仅存在于 chart/ 目录，paper/ 目录仍为旧版 fig2_framework.png（May 18）。需要：\n1. 将 fig2_framework_paperbanana.pdf 复制到 paper/ 目录\n2. 在 method.tex 中添加 figure 环境引用它\n\n## INFO: 新旧设计差异\n\n旧版 fig2 包含 Spearman ρ 柱状图（panel b），同时传达方法和数据。新版纯流程图，更清晰但信息密度降低。在 8 页限制下，旧版\"一图两用\"的设计可能更节省空间。这是设计决策，由 Director 判断。\n\n## 小问题\n- 图中 \"Mistral-7B\" 缺少 \"-v0.3\" 后缀（空间限制下可接受）\n- Worker 任务命名为 \"fig1\" 但做的是 fig2（任务命名混淆，不影响产出）"

[Reviewer 回复]:
[severity: warning] Figure 2 PaperBanana 重绘审查报告

## 产出确认
Worker 名为 "fig1-paperbanana" 但实际产出为 **fig2_framework_paperbanana.pdf/png**（17:25-17:31），是 Method 部分的 framework 图而非 Figure 1。

## 内容准确性 ✅
新图 3 个 panel 信息均正确：
- (a) Recursive Generation Chain: 8 LLMs, 5 families, 0.6B-8B, 方程 x^(k)=G(x^(k-1))
- (b) Feature Extraction: Pythia-410M → NLL Distribution → 5 NLL Statistics + 2 Surface → 7 DDR Features
- (c) LOGO Evaluation: 8 generators 正确列出，Mistral-7B 作为 held-out 示例，XGBoost → 3-class output

视觉质量好，排版干净，信息密度适中。

## WARNING: 图未集成到论文中

当前 method.tex **没有任何 \includegraphics 或 \begin{figure}**。新图仅存在于 chart/ 目录，paper/ 目录仍为旧版 fig2_framework.png（May 18）。需要：
1. 将 fig2_framework_paperbanana.pdf 复制到 paper/ 目录
2. 在 method.tex 中添加 figure 环境引用它

## INFO: 新旧设计差异

旧版 fig2 包含 Spearman ρ 柱状图（panel b），同时传达方法和数据。新版纯流程图，更清晰但信息密度降低。在 8 页限制下，旧版"一图两用"的设计可能更节省空间。这是设计决策，由 Director 判断。

## 小问题
- 图中 "Mistral-7B" 缺少 "-v0.3" 后缀（空间限制下可接受）
- Worker 任务命名为 "fig1" 但做的是 fig2（任务命名混淆，不影响产出）
